Task: task3_min_edges_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: zero_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 19
--- Model Response ---
To solve the problem, let's first identify the black nodes and the paths connecting them.

The black nodes are labeled as A and I.

Next, we need to identify all possible paths between nodes A and I:
1. A -> E -> B -> T -> F
2. A -> S -> B -> T -> F -> G -> J -> H -> I
3. A -> S -> B -> C -> G -> J -> H -> I
4. A -> E -> B -> C -> G -> J -> H -> I
5. A -> S -> B -> C -> G -> J -> O -> Q -> P -> I

To disconnect A and I, we need to remove edges from the above paths. The goal is to remove the minimum number of edges.

Notice that the paths converge at the edge (J, H). If we remove the edge (J, H), all paths going through node J that connect to node H will be cut off.

However, we need to consider the edge (O, Q) and (Q, P) which connects indirectly through Q to node I, bypassing node J.

To completely disallow any remaining connection paths, further edges (O, Q) and (Q, P) need to be removed.

Hence, the minimum number of edges that we need to remove, such that the two black nodes A and I are completely disconnected, are as follows:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(J, H), (O, Q), (Q, P)].
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 1, 'cut_set': [['H', 'I']]}

To solve this problem, we need to determine the minimum set of nodes that must be removed such that the two black nodes (S and O) are completely disconnected. Here is the step-by-step analysis:

1. **Identify the black nodes and their immediate neighbors:**
    - Black node S is connected to nodes A, E, and F.
    - Black node O is connected to node C.

2. **Trace the paths between the black nodes:**
    - Path from S to O via A, B, C: S -> A -> B -> C -> O
    - Path from S to O via E, B, C: S -> E -> B -> C -> O
    - Path from S to O via F, J, T, B, C: S -> F -> J -> T -> B -> C -> O
    - Path from S to O via F, J, Q: S -> F -> J -> Q -> H -> I -> G -> C -> O
    - Path from S to O via F, J, Q, I, G, C: S -> F -> J -> Q -> I -> G -> C -> O

3. **Identify nodes that, if removed, disconnect all paths:**
    - Removing node B disconnects the paths S -> A -> B -> C -> O and S -> E -> B -> C -> O.
    - Removing node C directly disconnects the paths S -> A -> B -> C -> O, S -> E -> B -> C -> O, and S -> F -> J -> Q -> H -> I -> G -> C -> O.
    - Removing node F disconnects the paths S -> F -> J -> T -> B -> C -> O and S -> F -> J -> Q -> H -> I -> G -> C -> O.
    - Removing node J disconnects the paths S -> F -> J -> T -> B -> C -> O and S -> F -> J -> Q -> H -> I -> G -> C -> O.

   However, removing either B or C disconnects both S and O entirely. Checking the intersections, we find that B and C are the minimal set that disconnect all paths.

   Checking all intermediate nodes, we can further determine that:

   - Removing node T blocks path via T-B.
   - Thus the nodes: T, B, and C is the minimum cut.
   
**Output Format:**

   - Node E
   - Node O

   - E is directly connected to S, B, and F.

3. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes for O:**
   - O is directly connected to J and Q.

4. **Continue to find the next level of connections:**
   - From S: connected to A, B, E
   - From B: connected to S, E, F, T, G, C, N
   - From F: connected to E, B, T
   - From J: connected to R, O, I, Q
   - From Q: connected to O, J, I, P

5. **Finding the common nodes (Naming the common nodes that appear in the neighbor sets):**
   - From Node E : B
   - From Node O: Q

6. **Exploring the common paths between these two nodes (E and O):**
   - E -> B -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> O (This path has a length of 5)
   - E -> B -> G -> C -> N -> E
   - E -> S  ->N
   - E -> B -> C -> N
   - E -> F -> T -> G -> C -> N
   - E -> B -> T -> G-> R
   - E -> B -> T -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> O (This is the blue path tested on before)
   - E -> B -> T -> F connected leads to back to point E

Therefore, E -> B -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> O is the shortest path between E and O with length 5.

Answer:

   - Removing node F would disconnect E and T, but we need to consider the minimum set of nodes.

     - Removing node J disconnects the remaining path E -> F -> Q -> J -> T.

   - The black nodes are labeled I and O.

2. **Find the immediate neighbors of the black nodes:**

   - Node O is connected to nodes J, G, C, and R.

   - Neighbors of I: H, Q, P
   - Neighbors of O: J, G, C, R

   - For node I:
     - H is connected to G and J.
     - Q is connected to P and R.
     - P is connected to Q.

     - C is connected to N, B, G, and O.
     - R is connected to Q, O, C, and N.

     - Path: I -> H -> G -> O

     This path has a length of 3.

   - I -> J -> G -> O: This is the same path length as above.
   - I -> Q -> R -> O: This connects through R to Q but since they are already checked, we see the longest path takes more than 3 edges.

Thus, the shortest path between I and O is:
